[Theatre seating 201-300]
The walls of this auditorium were built with amberwood, which when highly varnished, especially rebounds all sound, and makes for excellent acoustics. Natural reverberation, and delays can be produced by sounds travelling throughout the acoustical theatre. The theatre itself is fashioned like a giant wooden box, which grows larger towards the rear, naturally amplifying, and extending tones, especially from musical instruments, and vocals coming from center stage. A small door to the northeast leads to the performer's dressing room, and eventually center stage itself. Directly above you on the surface of the high ceiling have been painted scores of demons of different sizes and shapes and visages. Some are climbing onto the backs of ordinary citizens, who are in agonizing pain, and trying to claw out their eyes. Others fight with each other in grand displays of dark magic and mayhem, and a giant black long-clawed hand emerges from a desolate swirling void, which appears to be controlling them all like puppets hung from invisible strings attached to the dark deity's outstretched fingers of doom.

The grand theatre of Marr seats almost a thousand patrons at full capacity. Seats one through one hundred are in this section. The seating area of the auditorium is always kept immaculately clean, the floors pristine, and the plush red velvet padded chairs look brand new, and recline slightly, with drink holders on each padded arm rest. Large and spacious aisles line each section of seating, allowing for heavy traffic flow. The high ceiling draws your intent and gaze with wondrously painted murals of hundreds of different scenes and caricatures. In this northwestern corner section of the ceiling the many different planets, stars, solar systems, black holes, voids and quasars which rule the heavens of Aedin, are intricately portrayed in perfect alignment and scale.

Seats one hundred and one, through one hundred and five, and seats one hundred and ninety six, through two hundred are permanently reserved, and have each patron's name etched onto a silver name tag, which is fastened to the back of their respective seats. [Theatre seating 701-800]
From these seats, one has the best view of the extensive murals that have been painted across the high forward-vaulted ceiling. Various depictions of battle scenes, love scenes, deities, patrons, winter scenes, summer scenes, angels, actors, demons, and performers of all kinds have been painted beautifully and painstakingly, and with great detail. In the rear western corner of the ceiling is a massive mural of Aederon the all father reaching to the citizens of Aedin with love in his eyes, and in the rear eastern corner of the ceiling the mural becomes dark and twisted, as the ominous figure of Ty'ghat emerges from a desolate void. Directly above you their powers clash and cause conflict and strife between all beasts, beings, and men. In the center of the powerful deity clash, two beautiful and well-dressed Goddesses grapple and struggle to keep upright a large scale, which is the balance of life. The first Goddess is Eyridane, and she is dressed in white linen and royal-etched plates of the finest shining metal protecting her body and torso as armor. Her long cascading blond hair falls in perfect locks and curls below her shoulders. She wields a golden crossbow, and points it at the heart of darkness. The second Goddess wears crimson velvet and black arachis dress and armor respectively, and her wild untamed glossy black hair rambles down from her head in waves of blissful dark energy. She is Morana. She wields a long black battle fork which is pointed with defiance straight into the sun.

[Theatre of Marr-Grand Foyer]
l
The entrance to the world-renowned Marr theatre is brilliant to any eyes. Giant golden-chained crystal chandeliers cascade down from the three story heavily-textured ceiling in many prisms and arrays of light and crystal splendor. The red carpet which led you in here, now spreads out to cover almost the entire purple and blue marble floor. The marble walls and pillars in the entrance gleam of blues and purples mixed with pearl white into swirls of endless marbled patterns. Two red-carpeted marble staircases, one on each side of the room, curve their way elegantly up to a third story indoor balcony which overlooks the entire reception area. The exquisite handrails of the grand stairs are solid gold, and cast with golden vines and leaves intertwining between six-sided, and twisted rails which support the thick handrail itself. At the foot, and to the side of each staircase is a ten foot solid gold statue of a griffon with its massive wings splayed wide, and its regal hawkish head glaring at you as you pass by. The two grand stairs are always roped off with red velvet ropes and gold chains, unless there is a scheduled performance. Various tall and vibrant green broad-leaf plants are based in waist-high golden pots, and placed carefully throughout the grand foyer. To the north, two twelve foot solid oak doors with royal-carved patterns on them, swing outwards to reveal the gigantic auditorium within. A twenty five foot solid gold statue of a human, formally dressed, with a large top hat and a gentleman's cane, stands proudly as the centerpiece of the grand foyer. It is roped off with red-velvet ropes and gold chains, and a large golden-plated oak plaque at the bottom reads, "The Baron of Marr".

The area is illuminated.

In this room you see: Seren the Human
Obvious exits are: north, closed massive elmwood double doors

[Masquerade Main]
The theatre of Marr stands proudly here, three stories high, with outdoor balconies on each floor for the patrons to stand and socialize in between shows and events. A huge black cast-iron marquee with a gold-flaked billboard hangs from the roof halfway down the third floor. Tall well-lit golden letters at the top of the marquee read, "The theatre of Marr". The theatre facia itself is a testament to sculpting as each pillar, window casing, trim and balcony rail is etched, carved, and sculpted with designs of cherubs, angels, sad masks, happy masks, laughing masks, jokers, jesters, musicians and performers of all kinds. The grand entrance is four doors wide, and two stories high. The two double-doors are always opened, and red velvet ropes with gold chains adorn the sides of the double wide cobblestone walkway leading into the main theatre foyer. A long red carpet shows you the way in.

The area is illuminated.

[Masquerade Main]
The theatre of Marr stands proudly here, three stories high, with outdoor balconies on each floor for the patrons to stand and socialize in between shows and events. A huge black cast-iron marquee with a gold-flaked billboard hangs from the roof halfway down the third floor. Tall well-lit golden letters at the top of the marquee read, "The theatre of Marr". The theatre facia itself is a testament to sculpting as each pillar, window casing, trim and balcony rail is etched, carved, and sculpted with designs of cherubs, angels, sad masks, happy masks, laughing masks, jokers, jesters, musicians and performers of all kinds. The grand entrance is four doors wide, and two stories high. The two double-doors are always opened, and red velvet ropes with gold chains adorn the sides of the double wide cobblestone walkway leading into the main theatre foyer. A long red carpet shows you the way in.

The area is illuminated.

[Urda Town Square Center]
On this site, a fierce battle for control of the great city was waged, and on this site is where its citizens now celebrate that victory. Statues of the fallen heroes, and great leaders of the alliance adorn the tops of grand marble archways that scatter over the square. They stand ever vigilant against any unseen enemy. The people of Urda flock here to barter and trade, to converse with friends, or just relax near the fountain and have a game of chess. Benches and tables dot the landscape near the edge of the fountain. Hawkers of all type roll through this area with their wares, hoping to make a few coins. The many languages of races and guilds reverberate off the storefronts of the surrounding Merchant Square where a constant foot traffic ebbs and flows, making this the heart of the city.

The area is illuminated.

[Sanctuary of Eyridane and Morana]
The small path ends as the trees part, revealing a small clearing. Sunlight filters down from above, splashing the vibrant green grass and wildflowers. The noise of the central part of the grove all but fades away in this quiet sanctuary. An altar of stone rests in the center of the small clearing of verdant grass. Tangled vines have grown wild across the hard stone and though no markings can be seen, it is obvious that this is a place sacred to the twin goddesses of nature. Overhead the soft warbling of songbirds blends with the chirping crickets below into a sublime symphony of sounds. Small trinkets of adoration and worship have been left at the base of the altar, each one a symbol of devotion to their beloved gods.

The area is illuminated.

In this room are: simple stone altar
Obvious exits are: west, an arch of twisting branches

[Secluded Grove]
Trees have grown into a natural wall, protecting the clearing from any unwanted visitors. Vines have been woven through the tangled branches of the trees, blocking the rest of the forest from view. Soft grass peppered with patches of clover grows underfoot, and a small, brown rabbit nibbles contentedly on the green stalks.  A small group of sturdy Galirrim has gathered around a small spring of crystal clear water. [Tooth and Claw]
Banners of the Eyranas, stuffed boar heads, mammoth tusks, large limb and ribcage bones, other assorted bone-wear, and more Galirrim goods line the leather-trimmed walls and wooden shelves of this master tailor's shop made for lupines. In the very center of the room is a fifteen foot high, wood-carved statue of a Galirrim in mid-claw, his gaping maw open wide in full snarl. His wood fur and body have been recently stained with a deep brown coating which shines, and still smells of mineral spirits and oils. Bone and bead silkroot dream-catchers adorn one wall, and holy symbols of many shapes and sizes cover another. Boldly sticking out from the back wall is a large counter made of oak and tree stumps which has also been recently stained, and next to it stands a local Galirrim barking and growling to the Galirrim tailor's assistant in Ululari. They appear to be arguing over an item of small value which the local holds in his claws, and is displeased with. The Galirrim assistant is covered in Galirrim woads, which have been dyed into his fur in various colors, shapes, designs and hidden Galirrim meanings. As you approach, he tilts his head to one side and nods to you, and then returns to the disgruntled Galirrim local.

The area is illuminated.

In this room are: massive Galirrim statue
Obvious exits are: open towering pine door

[Urda Town Square Center]
On this site, a fierce battle for control of the great city was waged, and on this site is where its citizens now celebrate that victory. Statues of the fallen heroes, and great leaders of the alliance adorn the tops of grand marble archways that scatter over the square. They stand ever vigilant against any unseen enemy. The people of Urda flock here to barter and trade, to converse with friends, or just relax near the fountain and have a game of chess. Benches and tables dot the landscape near the edge of the fountain. Hawkers of all type roll through this area with their wares, hoping to make a few coins. The many languages of races and guilds reverberate off the storefronts of the surrounding Merchant Square where a constant foot traffic ebbs and flows, making this the heart of the city.

l at how on li
You examine the hyena-shaped Galirrim howler on Lilia...

A hyena skull has been hollowed out and cleaned. Covering the holes of the eyes, ears, and nose of this odd Galirrim instrument are small patches of boar skin anchored in with sinew. Several claw holes have been poked into the round part of the hyena skull, and the mouth is tied shut with sinew, while the teeth are all removed. To play this strange horn, a Galirrim places it to his maw, blows into the mouth end, covers and uncovers the skull holes with his claws, and it makes such a unique howling horn sound, that it tends to draw hyenas and wolves alike to the player, when properly played. Some say that male Galirrims also use these to woo the females. Etched into the jaw of the hyena skull is a canine tooth, and a claw.

[The Lonely Moon: Stars Club, Foyer]
The doors open into a quaint foyer with heavily polished cherrywood floors. Elegantly carved ebonwood pillars rise from the floor at the corners where the walls, papered in murals of detailed depictions of the night sky and its constellations, come together. A dimly lit, diamond and platinum-dusted chandelier hangs from the darkened ceiling like a bundle of shooting stars. Further down the corridor from the foyer is a heavy oak door, studded with silver stars and a lone, crescent moon painted in the center. A burly duskwen guards the back door. A cheerily lit side room to the west houses a myriad of unique clothing on mannequins, teak hangers, and cedarwood shelves, as well as glass counters filled with sparkling jewels and baubles. The eastern archway from the foyer leads into a cramped post office, dominated by a large counter in front of a vast array of tiny envelope cabinets.

[The Seaming Stars Tailor Shoppe]
A lovely hearth, crafted from sea stones and minor gems dominates the back center wall of the room. The mantle is draped in swathes of different colored and diversely textured fabrics. A large display window holds several mannequins on a deep, wide, ledge, artfully exhibited to catch the perfect position of light and atmosphere for the eyes of prospective customers. Unique glass bowls, each holding a distinctive, yet elegantly blended assortment of potpourri, are set about the room intermittently dispersing pleasing aromas. Varieties of fabrics have been neatly stacked in overhead cubbies, while jewels, gemstones, polished bone, and metals have been sorted by shape, size, and color behind locked cases and glass boxes. Each mannequin has a beautifully designed necklace about it, and other finished jewelry pieces have been tacked to placards indicating their price, or hung on knobbed pegs. Well-cobbled shoes and boots have been arrayed on clothed tables and cushioned stools. Brocade armchairs and full-length mirrors have been strategically placed throughout the store for the convenience of customers. A pearl and moonstone-beaded, moire-satin curtain separates the shop from the dressing room.

[The Seaming Stars Tailor Shoppe]
A lovely hearth, crafted from sea stones and minor gems dominates the back center wall of the room. The mantle is draped in swathes of different colored and diversely textured fabrics. A large display window holds several mannequins on a deep, wide, ledge, artfully exhibited to catch the perfect position of light and atmosphere for the eyes of prospective customers. Unique glass bowls, each holding a distinctive, yet elegantly blended assortment of potpourri, are set about the room intermittently dispersing pleasing aromas. Varieties of fabrics have been neatly stacked in overhead cubbies, while jewels, gemstones, polished bone, and metals have been sorted by shape, size, and color behind locked cases and glass boxes. Each mannequin has a beautifully designed necklace about it, and other finished jewelry pieces have been tacked to placards indicating their price, or hung on knobbed pegs. Well-cobbled shoes and boots have been arrayed on clothed tables and cushioned stools. Brocade armchairs and full-length mirrors have been strategically placed throughout the store for the convenience of customers. A pearl and moonstone-beaded, moire-satin curtain separates the shop from the dressing room.

The area is illuminated.

In this room you see: Preesha the Silvarin
Obvious exits are: east, closed beaded satin curtain

[The Lonely Moon: Stars Club, Foyer]
The doors open into a quaint foyer with heavily polished cherrywood floors. Elegantly carved ebonwood pillars rise from the floor at the corners where the walls, papered in murals of detailed depictions of the night sky and its constellations, come together. A dimly lit, diamond and platinum-dusted chandelier hangs from the darkened ceiling like a bundle of shooting stars. Further down the corridor from the foyer is a heavy oak door, studded with silver stars and a lone, crescent moon painted in the center. A burly duskwen guards the back door. A cheerily lit side room to the west houses a myriad of unique clothing on mannequins, teak hangers, and cedarwood shelves, as well as glass counters filled with sparkling jewels and baubles. The eastern archway from the foyer leads into a cramped post office, dominated by a large counter in front of a vast array of tiny envelope cabinets.

The area is illuminated.

[Western Cross Road]
Wagon marks mar the uneven cobbled road from so much use. Shops spring up on either side of the road, their owners gesturing for patrons to come in and try their new wares. A large Galirrim, towering above the other passersby, lumbers towards the town square in shreds of clothing. [Urda Town Square Center]
On this site, a fierce battle for control of the great city was waged, and on this site is where its citizens now celebrate that victory. Statues of the fallen heroes, and great leaders of the alliance adorn the tops of grand marble archways that scatter over the square. They stand ever vigilant against any unseen enemy. The people of Urda flock here to barter and trade, to converse with friends, or just relax near the fountain and have a game of chess. Benches and tables dot the landscape near the edge of the fountain. Hawkers of all type roll through this area with their wares, hoping to make a few coins. The many languages of races and guilds reverberate off the storefronts of the surrounding Merchant Square where a constant foot traffic ebbs and flows, making this the heart of the city.

[Western Cross Road]
A red brick storefront rises up two stories along the cobblestone road. Patrons bustle in and out of the shop which displays beautiful, one-of-a-kind, handcrafted clothing pieces and jewelry. Sounds of laughter and drinks being poured can be heard from inside as well, muffled behind the walls of the store. A gentleman usher politely opens the frosted glass double-doors for patrons as they pass through. Delicate silver stars have been filigreed into the glass of the doors, creating a backdrop for words shaped in gold thread stitch-lettering, "The Lonely Moon: Stars Club."

The area is illuminated.

In this room you see: Sleyd the Fae,  Kynea the Zacotl,  Preesha the Silvarin
Obvious exits are: west, east, open pair of frosted glass double doors

go door

[The Lonely Moon: Stars Club, Foyer]
The doors open into a quaint foyer with heavily polished cherrywood floors. Elegantly carved ebonwood pillars rise from the floor at the corners where the walls, papered in murals of detailed depictions of the night sky and its constellations, come together. A dimly lit, diamond and platinum-dusted chandelier hangs from the darkened ceiling like a bundle of shooting stars. Further down the corridor from the foyer is a heavy oak door, studded with silver stars and a lone, crescent moon painted in the center. A burly duskwen guards the back door. A cheerily lit side room to the west houses a myriad of unique clothing on mannequins, teak hangers, and cedarwood shelves, as well as glass counters filled with sparkling jewels and baubles. The eastern archway from the foyer leads into a cramped post office, dominated by a large counter in front of a vast array of tiny envelope cabinets.

The area is illuminated.

In this room you see: Sleyd the Fae,  Kynea the Zacotl,  Preesha the Silvarin, 1st burly, duskwen bouncer
Obvious exits are: west, open heavy oak door, archway, open pair of frosted glass double doors

w

[The Seaming Stars Tailor Shoppe]
A lovely hearth, crafted from sea stones and minor gems dominates the back center wall of the room. The mantle is draped in swathes of different colored and diversely textured fabrics. A large display window holds several mannequins on a deep, wide, ledge, artfully exhibited to catch the perfect position of light and atmosphere for the eyes of prospective customers. Unique glass bowls, each holding a distinctive, yet elegantly blended assortment of potpourri, are set about the room intermittently dispersing pleasing aromas. Varieties of fabrics have been neatly stacked in overhead cubbies, while jewels, gemstones, polished bone, and metals have been sorted by shape, size, and color behind locked cases and glass boxes. Each mannequin has a beautifully designed necklace about it, and other finished jewelry pieces have been tacked to placards indicating their price, or hung on knobbed pegs. Well-cobbled shoes and boots have been arrayed on clothed tables and cushioned stools. Brocade armchairs and full-length mirrors have been strategically placed throughout the store for the convenience of customers. A pearl and moonstone-beaded, moire-satin curtain separates the shop from the dressing room.

The area is illuminated.

In this room you see: Sleyd the Fae,  Kynea the Zacotl,  Preesha the Silvarin
Obvious exits are: east, open beaded satin curtain

You ask, "ky see anything?"

[The Lonely Moon: Stars Club, Foyer]
The doors open into a quaint foyer with heavily polished cherrywood floors. Elegantly carved ebonwood pillars rise from the floor at the corners where the walls, papered in murals of detailed depictions of the night sky and its constellations, come together. A dimly lit, diamond and platinum-dusted chandelier hangs from the darkened ceiling like a bundle of shooting stars. Further down the corridor from the foyer is a heavy oak door, studded with silver stars and a lone, crescent moon painted in the center. A burly duskwen guards the back door. A cheerily lit side room to the west houses a myriad of unique clothing on mannequins, teak hangers, and cedarwood shelves, as well as glass counters filled with sparkling jewels and baubles. The eastern archway from the foyer leads into a cramped post office, dominated by a large counter in front of a vast array of tiny envelope cabinets.

The area is illuminated.

[Merchant Square]
An eye-catching black striped tiger is emblazoned on the facade of lacquered wooden building on the south side of the square. Its shoulders raised high, and face frozen in a fierce roar. Through the many, high-arched, steel frame windows, a wooden figure can be seen wearing the latest gentleman's fashion. Grey smoke rises from a single stone chimney. A simple plaque hanging from a twisted wrought iron rod reads, "Regal Fashion". Smooth stone steps lead to the entrance. On the east side of the street a large brick chimney funnels white billowy smoke into the air projecting the aroma of fresh baked goods into the area. Through the large bay window a burly human man can be seen kneading and forming dough and placing his creations into an oven. The heavy mahogany door is propped seemingly propped to allow for relief from the ovens intense heat. A quaint barn faces the square from the west side of the street, its interior remodeled into a storefront, as seen through its large bay window.

[Urda Town Square Center]
On this site, a fierce battle for control of the great city was waged, and on this site is where its citizens now celebrate that victory. Statues of the fallen heroes, and great leaders of the alliance adorn the tops of grand marble archways that scatter over the square. They stand ever vigilant against any unseen enemy. The people of Urda flock here to barter and trade, to converse with friends, or just relax near the fountain and have a game of chess. Benches and tables dot the landscape near the edge of the fountain. Hawkers of all type roll through this area with their wares, hoping to make a few coins. The many languages of races and guilds reverberate off the storefronts of the surrounding Merchant Square where a constant foot traffic ebbs and flows, making this the heart of the city.

The area is illuminated.

In this room you see: Kynea the Zacotl,  Preesha the Silvarin,  Sleyd the Fae

A small shop constructed in black shingles attempts to blend into the shadows on the southern side of the street, its large wooden door beckoning patrons to enter. A small wooden sign posted above the door reads "Double Boil."

[Merchant Square]
An eye-catching black striped tiger is emblazoned on the facade of lacquered wooden building on the south side of the square. Its shoulders raised high, and face frozen in a fierce roar. Through the many, high-arched, steel frame windows, a wooden figure can be seen wearing the latest gentleman's fashion. Grey smoke rises from a single stone chimney. A simple plaque hanging from a twisted wrought iron rod reads, "Regal Fashion". Smooth stone steps lead to the entrance. On the east side of the street a large brick chimney funnels white billowy smoke into the air projecting the aroma of fresh baked goods into the area. Through the large bay window a burly human man can be seen kneading and forming dough and placing his creations into an oven. The heavy mahogany door is propped seemingly propped to allow for relief from the ovens intense heat. A quaint barn faces the square from the west side of the street, its interior remodeled into a storefront, as seen through its large bay window.

[Urda Town Square Center]
On this site, a fierce battle for control of the great city was waged, and on this site is where its citizens now celebrate that victory. Statues of the fallen heroes, and great leaders of the alliance adorn the tops of grand marble archways that scatter over the square. They stand ever vigilant against any unseen enemy. The people of Urda flock here to barter and trade, to converse with friends, or just relax near the fountain and have a game of chess. Benches and tables dot the landscape near the edge of the fountain. Hawkers of all type roll through this area with their wares, hoping to make a few coins. The many languages of races and guilds reverberate off the storefronts of the surrounding Merchant Square where a constant foot traffic ebbs and flows, making this the heart of the city.

The area is illuminated.

[Sanctuary of Eyridane and Morana]
The small path ends as the trees part, revealing a small clearing. Sunlight filters down from above, splashing the vibrant green grass and wildflowers. The noise of the central part of the grove all but fades away in this quiet sanctuary. An altar of stone rests in the center of the small clearing of verdant grass. Tangled vines have grown wild across the hard stone and though no markings can be seen, it is obvious that this is a place sacred to the twin goddesses of nature. Overhead the soft warbling of songbirds blends with the chirping crickets below into a sublime symphony of sounds. Small trinkets of adoration and worship have been left at the base of the altar, each one a symbol of devotion to their beloved gods.

The area is illuminated.

In this room are: simple stone altar
Obvious exits are: west, an arch of twisting branches

[Natures Evolution]
Twisted vines wrapped in ivy create a large circle in the center of the forested area. Several hollow trees sit askew on the outside border of the circle, large openings allowing for passage inside them. Several individuals dressed in green hooded robes stand beside their trees escorting prospective students in and out.

The area is illuminated.

Obvious exits are: west, an arch of twisting branches

[Eyranas Garden]
A small grotto green with life provides a place of peace and serenity.  A small wooden table made of thin planks of wood sits before two wooden benches on either side. A green candle burns casting the aroma of baby pine to the area. Neatly placed stone benches have been placed strategically among the elegant grotto proving resting places for parishioners. A small path which leads west provides exit into the main temple area.

The area is illuminated.

Obvious exits are: east, small path

go p

[Temple of the Eyranas]
Amazingly the interior of the temple is teaming with life. Birds fly freely amongst the tall trees and shrubs which give the appearance of the outside instead of in. Small animals scurry across the ground in an almost permanent state of play. A large fountain set in the center of the area spouts water high into the air which then settles back into the fountain without even the smallest splash. Statues of the Goddess's lie scattered among the various ponds and gardens of the temple. A small grassy pasture free of any growth takes its place on the northeastern side of the temple which sits beneath a beautiful green, moss covered altar. A gaggle of bushes trimmed to portray letters spells out the word "Life" in the northwestern corner of the temple. A small path that leads through some thick overgrowth to the west provides passage to another room. To the south an overgrown arch provides exit back to the street.

The area is illuminated.

In this room are: moss-covered green marble altar
Obvious exits are: small path
